<span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\EE(|S_n|) \\)</span></span></a></div></li>\n</ul>\n</li></ul>\n</li>\n</ul></nav></div>\n<main class=\"ptx-main\"><div id=\"ptx-content\" class=\"ptx-content\"><section xmlns:xlink=\"http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink\" class=\"chapter\" id=\"mines2020mp2\"><h2 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">2</span><span class=\"type\">Sujet</span><span class=\"title\">ccmp 2020, filière mp, Maths 2</span>\n</h2>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"mines2020mp2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-2-1\">L’objectif est d’étudier certains comportements asymptotiques dans une marche aléatoire. On y étudie le temps d’attente du premier retour vers l’origine ainsi que le nombre moyen de points visités par la marche. Le sujet ne se limite pas au cas d’une marche aléatoire sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb Z\\)</span> mais traite aussi du cas d’une marche dans le plan.</div></section><section class=\"exercises\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3\"><h3 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\"></span><span class=\"type\">Exercices</span><span class=\"title\">Énoncé et corrigé</span>\n</h3>\n<article class=\"warning remark-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-2\"><h4 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">2.1</span><span class=\"type\">Mise en garde</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span><span class=\"collapser button-box-custom\"> </span>\n</h4>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-2-1\">Les solutions ont été générées avec l’aide de DeepSeek-V3™, un modèle d’intelligence artificielle développé par DeepSeek. Elles seront clarifiées, voir corrigées pendant la séance dédiée.</div></article><section class=\"introduction\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-1\">Dans tout le texte, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(d\\)</span> est un élément de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{N}^*\\text{.}\\)</span> On note <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0_d\\)</span> le <span class=\"process-math\">\\(d\\)</span>-uplet dont toutes les coordonnées valent <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0\\text{,}\\)</span> c’est-à-dire le vecteur nul de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^d\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-2\">On considère une variable aléatoire <span class=\"process-math\">\\(X\\)</span> à valeurs dans <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{Z}^d\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\((X_k)_{k\n\\in \\mathbb{N}^*}\\)</span> une suite de variables aléatoires mutuellement indépendantes suivant chacune la loi de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(X\\)</span> et définies sur un même espace probabilisé. La suite de variables aléatoires <span class=\"process-math\">\\((S_n)_{n \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> est définie par <span class=\"process-math\">\\(S_0 = 0_d\\)</span> et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall n \\in \\mathbb{N}^*, \\quad S_n = \\sum_{k=1}^n X_k.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-4\">La suite <span class=\"process-math\">\\((S_n)_{n \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> est une <em class=\"emphasis\">marche aléatoire de pas</em> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(X\\text{,}\\)</span> à valeurs dans <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{Z}^d\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-5\">On note <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R\\)</span> la variable aléatoire à valeurs dans <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{N}^* \\cup \\{+\\infty\\}\\)</span> définie par</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nR = \\begin{cases}\n\\min\\{n \\in \\mathbb{N}^*, S_n = 0_d\\} \\amp \\text{si } \\{n \\in \\mathbb{N}^*, S_n = 0_d\\}\n\\neq \\emptyset, \\\\\n+\\infty \\amp \\text{sinon}.\n\\end{cases}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-7\">Autrement dit, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R\\)</span> est égal à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(+\\infty\\)</span> si la marche aléatoire <span class=\"process-math\">\\((S_n)_{n \\in\n\\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> ne revient jamais en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0_d\\text{,}\\)</span> au premier instant auquel cette marche aléatoire revient en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0_d\\)</span> sinon.</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-8\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\in \\mathbb{N}\\text{,}\\)</span> soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(N_n\\)</span> le cardinal du sous-ensemble</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\{S_k, k \\in \\{0, \\dots, n\\}\\}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-10\">de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{Z}^d\\text{.}\\)</span> Le nombre <span class=\"process-math\">\\(N_n\\)</span> est donc le nombre de points de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{Z}^d\\)</span> visités par la marche aléatoire <span class=\"process-math\">\\((S_n)_{n \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> après <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> pas.</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-11\">Le but du problème est d’étudier asymptotiquement l’espérance <span class=\"process-math\">\\(E(N_n)\\)</span> de la variable aléatoire <span class=\"process-math\">\\(N_n\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-3-12\">La partie D est indépendante des parties précédentes.</div></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">2.1.1</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Préliminaires</span>\n</h4>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-2\">Les cinq questions de cette partie sont indépendantes et utilisées dans les parties C et E.</div>\n<article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-3\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">1</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-3-1-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\in \\mathbb{N} \\text{.}\\)</span> En utilisant la factorisation <span class=\"process-math\">\\((X + 1)^{2n} =\n(X + 1)^n (X + 1)^n\\text{,}\\)</span> montrer que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=0}^n \\binom{n}{k}^2 = \\binom{2n}{n}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-3-2-1\">La factorisation <span class=\"process-math\">\\((X + 1)^{2n} = (X + 1)^n (X + 1)^n\\)</span> permet d’écrire :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=0}^{2n} \\binom{2n}{k} X^k = \\left( \\sum_{k=0}^n \\binom{n}{k} X^k \\right)\n\\left( \\sum_{k=0}^n \\binom{n}{k} X^k \\right).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-3-2-3\">En identifiant les coefficients de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(X^n\\)</span> des deux côtés, on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\binom{2n}{n} = \\sum_{k=0}^n \\binom{n}{k} \\binom{n}{n-k} = \\sum_{k=0}^n\n\\binom{n}{k}^2.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-4\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">2</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-4-1-1\">Rappeler la formule de Stirling, puis déterminer un nombre réel <span class=\"process-math\">\\(c \\gt 0 \\)</span> tel que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\binom{2n}{n} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} c \\frac{4^n}{\\sqrt{n}}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-4-2-1\">La formule de Stirling donne :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nn! \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\sqrt{2 \\pi n} \\left( \\frac{n}{e} \\right)^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-4-2-3\">En appliquant cette formule à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\binom{2n}{n}\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\binom{2n}{n} = \\frac{(2n)!}{(n!)^2} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim}\n\\frac{\\sqrt{4 \\pi n} \\left( \\frac{2n}{e} \\right)^{2n}}{2 \\pi n \\left(\n\\frac{n}{e} \\right)^{2n}} = \\frac{4^n}{\\sqrt{\\pi n}}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-4-2-5\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(c = \\frac{1}{\\sqrt{\\pi}} \\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">3</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5-1-1\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha \\)</span> est un élément de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(]0,1[ \\text{,}\\)</span> montrer, par exemple en utilisant une comparaison série-intégrale, que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=1}^n \\frac{1}{k^\\alpha} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim}\n\\frac{n^{1-\\alpha}}{1-\\alpha}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5-1-3\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha \\)</span> est un élément de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(]1,+\\infty[ \\text{,}\\)</span> montrer de même que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=n+1}^{+\\infty} \\frac{1}{k^\\alpha} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim}\n\\frac{1}{(\\alpha-1) n^{\\alpha-1}}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5-2-1\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha \\in ]0,1[ \\text{,}\\)</span> on utilise une comparaison série-intégrale. La fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f(x) = \\frac{1}{x^\\alpha} \\)</span> est décroissante, donc :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\int_1^{n+1} \\frac{1}{x^\\alpha} \\, dx \\leq \\sum_{k=1}^n \\frac{1}{k^\\alpha} \\leq\n1 + \\int_1^n \\frac{1}{x^\\alpha} \\, dx.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5-2-3\">En calculant l’intégrale, on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\int_1^{n+1} \\frac{1}{x^\\alpha} \\, dx = \\left[ \\frac{x^{1-\\alpha}}{1-\\alpha}\n\\right]_1^{n+1} = \\frac{(n+1)^{1-\\alpha} - 1}{1-\\alpha}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5-2-5\">On en déduit que :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=1}^n \\frac{1}{k^\\alpha} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim}\n\\frac{n^{1-\\alpha}}{1-\\alpha}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5-2-7\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha \\in ]1,+\\infty[ \\text{,}\\)</span> on utilise une comparaison similaire :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=n+1}^{+\\infty} \\frac{1}{k^\\alpha} \\leq \\int_n^{+\\infty}\n\\frac{1}{x^\\alpha} \\, dx = \\frac{1}{(\\alpha-1) n^{\\alpha-1}}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-5-2-9\">Ainsi, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=n+1}^{+\\infty} \\frac{1}{k^\\alpha} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim}\n\\frac{1}{(\\alpha-1) n^{\\alpha-1}}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">4</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6-1-1\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\in [2, +\\infty[ \\text{,}\\)</span> on pose</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nI(x) = \\int_2^x \\frac{dt}{\\ln(t)}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6-1-3\">Justifier, pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\in [2, +\\infty[ \\text{,}\\)</span> la relation</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nI(x) = \\frac{x}{\\ln(x)} - \\frac{2}{\\ln(2)} + \\int_2^x \\frac{dt}{(\\ln(t))^2}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6-1-5\">Établir par ailleurs la relation</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\int_2^x \\frac{dt}{(\\ln(t))^2} = o(I(x)).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6-1-7\">En déduire finalement un équivalent de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(I(x) \\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\)</span> tend vers <span class=\"process-math\">\\(+\\infty \\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6-2-1\">On intègre par parties en posant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u = \\frac{1}{\\ln(t)} \\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(dv = dt \\text{.}\\)</span> On obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nI(x) = \\left[ \\frac{t}{\\ln(t)} \\right]_2^x + \\int_2^x \\frac{dt}{(\\ln(t))^2} =\n\\frac{x}{\\ln(x)} - \\frac{2}{\\ln(2)} + \\int_2^x \\frac{dt}{(\\ln(t))^2}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6-2-3\">Pour montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\int_2^x \\frac{dt}{(\\ln(t))^2} = o(I(x)) \\text{,}\\)</span> on observe que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{1}{(\\ln(t))^2} \\)</span> est négligeable devant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{1}{\\ln(t)} \\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(t \\to +\\infty \\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, l’intégrale <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\int_2^x\n\\frac{dt}{(\\ln(t))^2} \\)</span> est négligeable devant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(I(x) \\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-6-2-4\">On en déduit que :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nI(x) \\underset{x \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\frac{x}{\\ln(x)}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-7\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">5</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-7-1-1\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha \\in \\mathbb{R} \\text{,}\\)</span> rappeler, sans donner de démonstration, le développement en série entière de <span class=\"process-math\">\\((1+x)^\\alpha \\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(]-1,1[ \\text{.}\\)</span> Justifier la formule :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in ]-1,1[, \\quad \\frac{1}{\\sqrt{1-x}} = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty}\n\\frac{(2n)}{4^n} x^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-7-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-7-2-1\">Le développement en série entière de <span class=\"process-math\">\\((1+x)^\\alpha \\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(]-1,1[ \\)</span> est :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n(1+x)^\\alpha = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} \\binom{\\alpha}{n} x^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-7-2-3\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha = -\\frac{1}{2} \\text{,}\\)</span> on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{1}{\\sqrt{1-x}} = (1-x)^{-\\frac{1}{2}} = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty}\n\\binom{-\\frac{1}{2}}{n} (-x)^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-7-2-5\">En utilisant la formule des coefficients binomiaux généralisés, on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\binom{-\\frac{1}{2}}{n} = \\frac{(-1)^n (2n)}{4^n n!},\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-4-7-2-7\">d’où :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{1}{\\sqrt{1-x}} = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} \\frac{(2n)}{4^n} x^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">2.1.2</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Marches aléatoires, récurrence</span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-2-1\">On considère les fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G\\)</span> définies par les formules</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in ]-1, 1[, \\quad F(x) = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} P(S_n = 0_d) \\, x^n;\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in ]-1, 1[, \\quad G(x) = \\sum_{n=1}^{+\\infty} P(R = n) \\, x^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-3\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">6</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-3-1-1\">Montrer que les séries entières définissant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G\\)</span> ont un rayon de convergence supérieur ou égal à 1. Justifier alors que les fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G\\)</span> sont définies et de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C^\\infty\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(]-1,1[\\text{.}\\)</span> Montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G\\)</span> est définie et continue sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\([-1,1]\\)</span> et que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nG(1) = P(R \\neq +\\infty).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-3-2-1\">Les séries entières définissant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G\\)</span> sont de la forme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty}\na_n x^n\\)</span> avec <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a_n \\leq 1\\text{.}\\)</span> Par conséquent, leur rayon de convergence est au moins 1. Les fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G\\)</span> sont donc définies et de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C^\\infty\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(]-1,1[\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-3-2-2\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G\\text{,}\\)</span> la série converge en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x = 1\\)</span> car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G(1) =\n\\sum_{n=1}^{+\\infty} P(R = n) = P(R \\neq +\\infty)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-4\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">7</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-4-1-1\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(k\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> sont des entiers naturels non nuls tels que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(k \\leq n\\text{,}\\)</span> montrer que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP((S_n = 0_d) \\cap (R = k)) = P(R = k) P(S_{n-k} = 0_d).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-4-1-3\">En déduire que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall n \\in \\mathbb{N}^*, \\quad P(S_n = 0_d) = \\sum_{k=1}^n P(R = k) P(S_{n-k}\n= 0_d).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-4-2-1\">L’événement <span class=\"process-math\">\\((S_n = 0_d) \\cap (R = k)\\)</span> signifie que la marche revient en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0_d\\)</span> pour la première fois à l’instant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(k\\text{,}\\)</span> puis revient à nouveau en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0_d\\)</span> à l’instant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\text{.}\\)</span> Par indépendance des pas, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP((S_n = 0_d) \\cap (R = k)) = P(R = k) P(S_{n-k} = 0_d).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-4-2-3\">En sommant sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(k\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(S_n = 0_d) = \\sum_{k=1}^n P(R = k) P(S_{n-k} = 0_d).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">8</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-1-1\">Montrer que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in ]-1,1[, \\quad F(x) = 1 + F(x) G(x).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-1-3\">Déterminer la limite de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F(x)\\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\to 1^-\\text{,}\\)</span> en discutant selon la valeur de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R \\neq +\\infty)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2-1\">En utilisant la relation de convolution des probabilités, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nF(x) = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} P(S_n = 0_d) x^n = 1 + \\sum_{n=1}^{+\\infty}\n\\sum_{k=1}^n P(R = k) P(S_{n-k} = 0_d) x^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2-3\">En réorganisant les termes, on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nF(x) = 1 + F(x) G(x).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2-5\">Pour la limite de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F(x)\\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\to 1^-\\text{,}\\)</span> on distingue deux cas :</div>\n<ul class=\"disc\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2-6\">\n<li id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2-6-1\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2-6-1\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R \\neq +\\infty) \\lt 1\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F(x)\\)</span> converge vers une valeur finie.</div></li>\n<li id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2-6-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-mines2020mp2-3-5-5-2-6-2\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R \\neq +\\infty) = 1\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(F(x)\\)</span> diverge vers <span class=\"process-math\">\\(+\\infty\\)</span> .</div></li>\n</ul>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-6\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">9</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-6-1-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\((c_k)_{k \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> une suite d’éléments de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^+\\)</span> telle que la série entière <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum c_k x^k\\)</span> ait un rayon de convergence 1 et que la série <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum c_k\\)</span> diverge. Montrer que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=0}^{+\\infty} c_k x^k \\underset{x \\to 1^-}{\\longrightarrow} +\\infty.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-6-1-3\"><em class=\"emphasis\">L’élément <span class=\"process-math\">\\(A\\)</span> de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^{++}\\)</span> étant fixé, on montrera qu’il existe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha \\in ]0,1[\\)</span> tel que</em></div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in ]1-\\alpha,1[, \\quad \\sum_{k=0}^{+\\infty} c_k x^k \\gt A.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-6-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-6-2-1\">La série <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum c_k\\)</span> diverge, donc pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(A \\gt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> il existe un entier <span class=\"process-math\">\\(N\\)</span> tel que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum_{k=0}^N c_k \\gt A\\text{.}\\)</span> Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\in\n]1-\\alpha,1[\\text{,}\\)</span> on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\sum_{k=0}^{+\\infty} c_k x^k \\geq \\sum_{k=0}^N c_k x^k \\geq \\sum_{k=0}^N c_k\n(1-\\alpha)^k.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-6-2-3\">En choisissant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha\\)</span> suffisamment petit, on peut garantir que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum_{k=0}^N\nc_k (1-\\alpha)^k \\gt A\\text{,}\\)</span> ce qui montre que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum_{k=0}^{+\\infty} c_k x^k\n\\to +\\infty\\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\to 1^-\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-7\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">10</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-7-1-1\">Montrer que la série <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum P(S_n = 0_d)\\)</span> est divergente si et seulement si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R\n\\neq +\\infty) = 1\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-7-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-7-2-1\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R \\neq +\\infty) = 1\\text{,}\\)</span> alors la marche revient presque sûrement en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0_d\\)</span> une infinité de fois, ce qui implique que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum P(S_n = 0_d)\\)</span> diverge. Réciproquement, si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\sum P(S_n = 0_d)\\)</span> diverge, alors la marche revient presque sûrement en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0_d\\text{,}\\)</span> donc <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R \\neq +\\infty) = 1\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-8\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">11</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-8-1-1\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(i \\in \\mathbb{N}^*\\text{,}\\)</span> soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(Y_i\\)</span> la variable de Bernoulli indicatrice de l’événement</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Big(S_i \\notin \\{S_k, 0 \\leq k \\leq i-1\\}\\Big).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-8-1-3\">Montrer que, pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(i \\in \\mathbb{N}^*\\)</span> :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(Y_i = 1) = P(R \\gt i).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-8-1-5\">En déduire que, pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\in \\mathbb{N}^*\\)</span> :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nE(N_n) = 1 + \\sum_{i=1}^n P(R \\gt i).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-8-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-8-2-1\">L’événement <span class=\"process-math\">\\(Y_i = 1\\)</span> correspond au fait que la marche visite un nouveau point à l’instant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(i\\text{,}\\)</span> ce qui équivaut à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R \\gt i\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(Y_i =\n1) = P(R \\gt i)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-8-2-2\">En sommant sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(i\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nE(N_n) = 1 + \\sum_{i=1}^n P(R \\gt i).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-9\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">12</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-9-1-1\">Conclure que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{E(N_n)}{n} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\longrightarrow} P(R = +\\infty).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-9-1-3\"><em class=\"emphasis\">On pourra admettre et utiliser le théorème de Cesaro : si <span class=\"process-math\">\\((u_n)_{n \\in\n\\mathbb{N}^*}\\)</span> est une suite réelle convergeant vers le nombre réel <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\ell\\text{,}\\)</span> alors</em></div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{1}{n} \\sum_{k=1}^n u_k \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\longrightarrow} \\ell.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-9-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-9-2-1\">Par le théorème de Cesaro, si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R \\gt i) \\to P(R = +\\infty)\\text{,}\\)</span> alors :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{1}{n} \\sum_{i=1}^n P(R \\gt i) \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\longrightarrow}\nP(R = +\\infty).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-5-9-2-3\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{E(N_n)}{n} \\to P(R = +\\infty)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">2.1.3</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Les marches de Bernoulli sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{Z}\\)</span></span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-2-1\">Dans cette question, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(d\\)</span> est égal à 1 et on note donc simplement <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0_d = 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Par ailleurs, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(p\\)</span> est un élément de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(]0,1[\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(q = 1 - p\\)</span> et la loi de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(X\\)</span> est donnée par</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(X = 1) = p \\quad \\text{et} \\quad P(X = -1) = q.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-3\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">13</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-3-1-1\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\in \\mathbb{N}\\text{,}\\)</span> déterminer <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(S_{2n+1} = 0)\\)</span> et justifier l’égalité :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(S_{2n} = 0) = \\binom{2n}{n} (pq)^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-3-2-1\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\in \\mathbb{N}\\text{,}\\)</span> la marche ne peut pas revenir à l’origine en un nombre impair de pas, donc <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(S_{2n+1} = 0) = 0\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-3-2-2\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(S_{2n} = 0)\\text{,}\\)</span> on utilise le fait que la marche doit faire autant de pas à droite qu’à gauche. Le nombre de chemins possibles est <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\binom{2n}{n}\\text{,}\\)</span> et chaque chemin a une probabilité <span class=\"process-math\">\\((pq)^n\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(S_{2n} = 0) = \\binom{2n}{n} (pq)^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-4\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">14</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-4-1-1\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\in ]-1,1[\\text{,}\\)</span> donner une expression simple de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G(x)\\text{.}\\)</span> Exprimer <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R\n= +\\infty)\\)</span> en fonction de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(|p - q|\\text{.}\\)</span> Déterminer la loi de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-4-2-1\">La fonction génératrice <span class=\"process-math\">\\(G(x)\\)</span> est donnée par :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nG(x) = \\sum_{n=1}^{+\\infty} P(R = n) x^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-4-2-3\">Pour une marche de Bernoulli symétrique (<span class=\"process-math\">\\(p = q = \\frac{1}{2}\\)</span>), on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nG(x) = 1 - \\sqrt{1 - 4pqx^2}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-4-2-5\">La probabilité que la marche ne revienne jamais à l’origine est :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(R = +\\infty) = 1 - G(1) = |p - q|.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-4-2-7\">La loi de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R\\)</span> est une loi géométrique modifiée, avec <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R = n) =\n\\frac{1}{2n-1} \\binom{2n}{n} (pq)^n\\)</span> pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\geq 1\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-5\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">15</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-5-1-1\">On suppose que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(p = q = \\frac{1}{2}\\text{.}\\)</span> Donner un équivalent simple de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(P(R\n= 2n)\\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> tend vers <span class=\"process-math\">\\(+\\infty\\text{.}\\)</span> En déduire un équivalent simple de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(E(N_n)\\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> tend vers <span class=\"process-math\">\\(+\\infty\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-5-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-5-2-1\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(p = q = \\frac{1}{2}\\text{,}\\)</span> on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(R = 2n) = \\frac{1}{2n-1} \\binom{2n}{n} \\left(\\frac{1}{4}\\right)^n.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-5-2-3\">En utilisant la formule de Stirling, on obtient l’équivalent :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(R = 2n) \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\frac{1}{\\sqrt{\\pi n^3}}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-6-5-2-5\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(E(N_n)\\text{,}\\)</span> on utilise le résultat de la question 12 :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nE(N_n) \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\sqrt{\\frac{8n}{\\pi}}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">2.1.4</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Un résultat asymptotique</span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-2-1\">Soient <span class=\"process-math\">\\((a_n)_{n \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\((b_n)_{n \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> deux suites d’éléments de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^{++}\\text{.}\\)</span> On suppose que <span class=\"process-math\">\\((a_n)_{n \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> est décroissante et que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall n \\in \\mathbb{N}, \\quad \\sum_{k=0}^n a_k b_{n-k} = 1.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-2-3\">On pose, pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\in \\mathbb{N}\\)</span> :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nB_n = \\sum_{k=0}^n b_k.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-3\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">16</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-3-1-1\">Soient <span class=\"process-math\">\\(m\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> deux entiers naturels tels que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(m \\gt n\\text{.}\\)</span> Montrer que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\na_n \\leq \\frac{1}{B_n} \\quad \\text{et} \\quad 1 \\leq a_n B_{m-n} + a_0 (B_m -\nB_{m-n}).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-3-2-1\">Par définition, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(B_n = \\sum_{k=0}^n b_k\\text{.}\\)</span> Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a_n\\)</span> est décroissante, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\na_n \\leq \\frac{1}{B_n}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-3-2-3\">Pour la deuxième inégalité, on utilise la relation de convolution :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n1 = \\sum_{k=0}^n a_k b_{n-k} \\leq a_n B_{m-n} + a_0 (B_m - B_{m-n}).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-4\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">17</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-4-1-1\">On suppose dans cette question qu’il existe une suite <span class=\"process-math\">\\((m_n)_{n \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> vérifiant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(m_n \\gt n\\)</span> pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> assez grand et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nB_{m_n - n} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} B_n \\quad \\text{et} \\quad B_{m_n} -\nB_{m_n - n} \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\longrightarrow} 0.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-4-1-3\">Montrer que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\na_n \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\frac{1}{B_n}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-4-2-1\">En utilisant les hypothèses, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\na_n \\leq \\frac{1}{B_n} \\quad \\text{et} \\quad 1 \\leq a_n B_{m_n - n} + a_0\n(B_{m_n} - B_{m_n - n}).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-4-2-3\">Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(B_{m_n - n} \\sim B_n\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(B_{m_n} - B_{m_n - n} \\to 0\\text{,}\\)</span> on en déduit que :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\na_n \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\frac{1}{B_n}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-5\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">18</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-5-1-1\">On suppose dans cette question qu’il existe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C \\gt 0\\)</span> tel que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nb_n \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\frac{C}{n}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-5-1-3\">En utilisant la question 17 pour une suite <span class=\"process-math\">\\((m_n)_{n \\in \\mathbb{N}}\\)</span> bien choisie, montrer que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\na_n \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\frac{1}{C \\ln(n)}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-5-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-5-2-1\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(b_n \\sim \\frac{C}{n}\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(B_n \\sim C \\ln(n)\\text{.}\\)</span> En choisissant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(m_n\n= 2n\\text{,}\\)</span> on vérifie que :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nB_{m_n - n} = B_n \\sim C \\ln(n) \\quad \\text{et} \\quad B_{m_n} - B_{m_n - n} \\sim\nC \\ln(2n) - C \\ln(n) = C \\ln(2).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-7-5-2-3\">Ainsi, d’après la question 17, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\na_n \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\frac{1}{C \\ln(n)}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">2.1.5</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">La marche aléatoire simple sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{Z}^2\\)</span></span>\n</h4>\n<article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-2\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">19</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-2-1-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\in \\mathbb{N}^*\\text{.}\\)</span> Montrer que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n1 = \\sum_{k=0}^n P(S_k = 0_d) P(R \\gt n - k).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-2-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-2-2-1\">Cette égalité découle de la propriété de convolution des probabilités et de l’indépendance des pas de la marche aléatoire. En sommant sur tous les instants <span class=\"process-math\">\\(k\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n1 = \\sum_{k=0}^n P(S_k = 0_d) P(R \\gt n - k).\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-3\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">20</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-3-1-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\in \\mathbb{N}\\text{.}\\)</span> Établir l’égalité</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(S_{2n} = 0_2) = \\left( \\frac{\\binom{2n}{n}}{4^n} \\right)^2.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-3-2-1\">Pour une marche aléatoire simple sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{Z}^2\\text{,}\\)</span> la probabilité de revenir à l’origine après <span class=\"process-math\">\\(2n\\)</span> pas est donnée par :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nP(S_{2n} = 0_2) = \\left( \\frac{\\binom{2n}{n}}{4^n} \\right)^2.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-3-2-3\">Ce résultat découle de la symétrie de la marche et de la formule des coefficients binomiaux.</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-4\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">21</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-4-1-1\">Donner un équivalent simple de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(E(N_n)\\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> tend vers <span class=\"process-math\">\\(+\\infty\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-4-2-1\">Pour une marche aléatoire simple sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{Z}^2\\text{,}\\)</span> on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nE(N_n) \\underset{n \\to +\\infty}{\\sim} \\frac{\\pi n}{\\ln(n)}.\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"mines2020mp2-3-8-4-2-3\">Ce résultat est un théorème classique dû à Erdős et Dvoretzky.</div>\n</div></details></div></article></section></section></section></div>\n<div class=\"ptx-content-footer\">\n<a class=\"previous-button button\" href=\"centrale2018mp2.html\" class=\"ptx-content\"><section xmlns:xlink=\"http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink\" class=\"chapter\" id=\"centrale2018mp2\"><h2 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1</span><span class=\"type\">Sujet</span><span class=\"title\">centrale 2018, filière mp, Maths 2</span>\n</h2>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-2-1\">L’objectif est d’étudier les fonctions dites harmoniques et de démontrer le principe du maximum pour celles-ci. Plusieurs applications en sont proposées : une démonstration du théorème fondamental de l’algèbre et la résolution du problème dit de Dirichlet.</div></section><section class=\"exercises\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3\"><h3 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\"></span><span class=\"type\">Exercices</span><span class=\"title\">Énoncé et corrigé</span>\n</h3>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-2-1\">Ce problème étudie quelques propriétés des fonctions harmoniques ainsi que quelques exemples de telles fonctions (parties I et II). Dans la partie III, largement indépendante du reste du problème, on montre le principe du maximum faible pour le laplacien. Dans la partie IV, on établit un lien entre les fonctions harmoniques de deux variables et les fonctions développables en série entière, et on propose la résolution du problème de Dirichlet dans le disque unité de ℝ² dans la partie V.</div></section><section class=\"paragraphs\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3\"><h4 class=\"heading\"><span class=\"title\">Notations.</span></h4>\n<ul class=\"disc\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2\">\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-1\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-1\">Dans ce préambule et dans les parties I et III, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> désigne un entier strictement positif.</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-2\">On munit ℝⁿ de sa structure euclidienne canonique et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\|\\cdot\\|\\)</span> désigne la norme euclidienne.</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-3\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-3\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> est une partie de ℝⁿ, alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\)</span> désigne son adhérence et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\partial U\\)</span> sa frontière.</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-4\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-4\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a \\in \\mathbb{R}^n\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R \\gt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> on désigne par <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(a, R)\\)</span> la boule ouverte de centre <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a\\)</span> et de rayon <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R\\)</span> pour la distance euclidienne. Autrement dit<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nD(a, R) = \\{x \\in \\mathbb{R}^n ; \\|x - a\\| \\lt R\\}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>La boule fermée de centre <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a\\)</span> et de rayon <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R\\)</span> est alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{D(a, R)}\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-5\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-5\">L’opérateur différentiel <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta\\)</span> (appelé laplacien) est défini pour toute fonction à valeurs réelles de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur un ouvert <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U \\subset\n\\mathbb{R}^n\\)</span> par<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x = (x_1, \\dots, x_n) \\in U, \\quad \\Delta f(x) =\n\\sum_{i=1}^n \\frac{\\partial^2 f}{\\partial x_i^2}(x)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-6\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-6\">Une fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> à valeurs réelles sur un ouvert <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> de ℝⁿ est dite harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> si<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in U, \\quad\n\\Delta f(x) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>L’ensemble des fonctions harmoniques sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> est noté <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></li>\n</ul></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.1</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques : quelques propriétés</span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-2-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> un ouvert non vide de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^n\\text{.}\\)</span> On note <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2(U,\n\\mathbb{R})\\)</span> l’espace vectoriel des fonctions de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> dans <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-3\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">1</span><span class=\"title\">La première question.</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-3-2-1\">Montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\)</span> est un sous-espace vectoriel de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2(U,\n\\mathbb{R})\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-3-3\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-3-3-1\">On a <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U) \\subset \\mathcal{C}^2(U, \\mathbb{R})\\text{.}\\)</span> La fonction nulle est harmonique. Soient <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f, g \\in \\mathcal{H}(U)\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\n\\in\n\\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f + \\lambda g\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2(U,\n\\mathbb{R})\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\forall i \\in [\\![1, n]\\!]\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial^2}{\\partial\nx_i^2}\\)</span> est linéaire, donc <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta(f + \\lambda g) = \\Delta(f) + \\lambda\n\\Delta(g) = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f + \\lambda g \\in \\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\)</span> est un sous-espace vectoriel de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2(U,\n\\mathbb{R})\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-4\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">2</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-4-1-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f \\in \\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Montrer que si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^\\infty\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{,}\\)</span> alors toute dérivée partielle à tout ordre de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> appartient à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">On suppose que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f \\in \\mathcal{C}^2(U, \\mathbb{R}) \\cap \\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(p \\in \\mathbb{N}\\text{,}\\)</span> posons <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial^p f}{\\partial x_{i_1}\n\\partial x_{i_2} \\dots \\partial x_{i_p}} = g\\text{.}\\)</span> Par application du théorème de Schwartz et de la linéarité de l’opérateur dérivée partielle, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta(g)\n= \\sum_{j=1}^n \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial x_j^2} =\n\\frac{\\partial^p}{\\partial\nx_{i_1} \\partial x_{i_2} \\dots \\partial x_{i_p}} \\left( \\sum_{j=1}^n\n\\frac{\\partial^2 f}{\\partial x_j^2} \\right) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Donc <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g \\in\n\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-5\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">3</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-5-1-1\">On suppose dans cette question que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> est connexe par arcs. Déterminer l’ensemble des fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\)</span> telles que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f^2\\)</span> appartienne aussi à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-5-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-5-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f \\in \\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{,}\\)</span> alors pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(i \\in [\\![1, n]\\!]\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial\nf^2}{\\partial x_i} = 2f \\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial x_i}\\text{.}\\)</span> En dérivant une seconde fois, on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial^2 f^2}{\\partial x_i^2} = 2\n\\left(\n\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial x_i} \\right)^2 + 2f \\frac{\\partial^2\nf}{\\partial\nx_i^2}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Donc :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta(f^2) = 0 \\iff \\sum_{i=1}^n \\left(\n\\frac{\\partial\nf}{\\partial x_i} \\right)^2 + f \\Delta(f) = 0 \\iff \\sum_{i=1}^n \\left(\n\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial x_i} \\right)^2 = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f \\in\n\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\forall i \\in [\\![1, n]\\!]\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial\nf}{\\partial x_i} = 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^1\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{,}\\)</span> elle est différentiable sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{,}\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> étant connexe par arcs, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est constante sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-6\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">4</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-6-1-1\">Donner une fonction non constante appartenant à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Le produit de deux fonctions harmoniques est-il une fonction harmonique ?</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-6-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-6-2-1\">L’application <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f : x = (x_1, \\dots, x_n) \\mapsto x_1\\)</span> est harmonique, mais <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f^2\\)</span> ne l’est pas. Ainsi, le produit de deux fonctions harmoniques sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> n’est pas nécessairement harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Exemples de fonctions harmoniques</span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2.1</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques à variables séparables</span>\n</h5>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-2-1\">On cherche dans cette question à déterminer les fonctions harmoniques non nulles sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^2\\)</span> à variables séparables, c’est-à-dire les fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> s’écrivant sous la forme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f(x, y) = u(x)v(y)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">5</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-3-1-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Montrer qu’il existe une constante <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\)</span> réelle telle que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> soient solutions respectives des équations</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz'' + \\lambda z =\n0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz'' - \\lambda z = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\((x, y) \\in \\mathbb{R}^2\\text{,}\\)</span> on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta(f)(x, y) = u''(x)v(y)\n+ u(x)v''(y)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est harmonique, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta(f)(x, y) = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> donc :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nu''(x)v(y) + u(x)v''(y) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En divisant par <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u(x)v(y)\\)</span> (qui est non nul car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> n’est pas identiquement nulle), on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{u''(x)}{u(x)}\n+ \\frac{v''(y)}{v(y)} = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Cela implique qu’il existe une constante <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\)</span> telle que :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{u''(x)}{u(x)} = -\\lambda \\quad \\text{et}\n\\quad \\frac{v''(y)}{v(y)} = \\lambda\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> sont solutions des équations différentielles données.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">6</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-1-1\">Donner en fonction du signe de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\)</span> la forme des fonctions harmoniques à variables séparables.</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Selon le signe de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\text{,}\\)</span> les solutions sont :</div>\n<ul class=\"disc\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2\">\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-1\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-1\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de la forme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f(x, y) =\n(a x + b)(c y + d)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-2\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda \\gt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de la forme :<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x,\ny) = \\left( a \\cos(\\sqrt{\\lambda} x) + b \\sin(\\sqrt{\\lambda} x) \\right)\n\\left( c e^{\\sqrt{\\lambda} y} + d e^{-\\sqrt{\\lambda} y} \\right)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-3\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-3\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda \\lt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de la forme :<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x,\ny) = \\left( a e^{\\sqrt{-\\lambda} x} + b e^{-\\sqrt{-\\lambda} x} \\right)\n\\left( c \\cos(\\sqrt{-\\lambda} y) + d \\sin(\\sqrt{-\\lambda} y)\n\\right)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a, b, c, d\\)</span> sont des constantes arbitraires.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2.2</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques radiales</span>\n</h5>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-2\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> une fonction réelle de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^2\n\\setminus \\{(0, 0)\\}\\text{.}\\)</span> On pose, pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\((r, \\theta) \\in \\mathbb{R}^+\n\\times \\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\ng(r, \\theta) = f(r \\cos(\\theta), r \\sin(\\theta))\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">7</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-3-1-1\">Justifier que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^+\n\\times \\mathbb{R}\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-3-2-1\">L’application <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h : (r, \\theta) \\mapsto (r \\cos(\\theta), r\n\\sin(\\theta))\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^+\n\\times \\mathbb{R}\\)</span> car ses composantes le sont. Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g = f \\circ\nh\\text{,}\\)</span> et que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> est également de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-4\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">8</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-4-1-1\">Pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\((r, \\theta) \\in \\mathbb{R}^+ \\times \\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span> exprimer <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial\ng}{\\partial r}(r, \\theta)\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial g}{\\partial\n\\theta}(r,\n\\theta)\\)</span> en fonction des id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-6\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">10</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-6-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">En utilisant les expressions des dérivées partielles de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> obtenues précédemment, on a :</div>\n<div
