<span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\EE(|S_n|) \\)</span></span></a></div></li>\n</ul>\n</li></ul>\n</li>\n</ul></nav></div>\n<main class=\"ptx-main\"><div id=\"ptx-content\" class=\"ptx-content\"><section xmlns:xlink=\"http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink\" class=\"chapter\" id=\"centrale2018mp2\"><h2 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1</span><span class=\"type\">Sujet</span><span class=\"title\">centrale 2018, filière mp, Maths 2</span>\n</h2>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-2-1\">L’objectif est d’étudier les fonctions dites harmoniques et de démontrer le principe du maximum pour celles-ci. Plusieurs applications en sont proposées : une démonstration du théorème fondamental de l’algèbre et la résolution du problème dit de Dirichlet.</div></section><section class=\"exercises\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3\"><h3 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\"></span><span class=\"type\">Exercices</span><span class=\"title\">Énoncé et corrigé</span>\n</h3>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-2-1\">Ce problème étudie quelques propriétés des fonctions harmoniques ainsi que quelques exemples de telles fonctions (parties I et II). Dans la partie III, largement indépendante du reste du problème, on montre le principe du maximum faible pour le laplacien. Dans la partie IV, on établit un lien entre les fonctions harmoniques de deux variables et les fonctions développables en série entière, et on propose la résolution du problème de Dirichlet dans le disque unité de ℝ² dans la partie V.</div></section><section class=\"paragraphs\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3\"><h4 class=\"heading\"><span class=\"title\">Notations.</span></h4>\n<ul class=\"disc\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2\">\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-1\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-1\">Dans ce préambule et dans les parties I et III, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\\)</span> désigne un entier strictement positif.</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-2\">On munit ℝⁿ de sa structure euclidienne canonique et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\|\\cdot\\|\\)</span> désigne la norme euclidienne.</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-3\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-3\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> est une partie de ℝⁿ, alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\)</span> désigne son adhérence et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\partial U\\)</span> sa frontière.</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-4\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-4\">Pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a \\in \\mathbb{R}^n\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R \\gt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> on désigne par <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(a, R)\\)</span> la boule ouverte de centre <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a\\)</span> et de rayon <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R\\)</span> pour la distance euclidienne. Autrement dit<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nD(a, R) = \\{x \\in \\mathbb{R}^n ; \\|x - a\\| \\lt R\\}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>La boule fermée de centre <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a\\)</span> et de rayon <span class=\"process-math\">\\(R\\)</span> est alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{D(a, R)}\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-5\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-5\">L’opérateur différentiel <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta\\)</span> (appelé laplacien) est défini pour toute fonction à valeurs réelles de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur un ouvert <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U \\subset\n\\mathbb{R}^n\\)</span> par<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x = (x_1, \\dots, x_n) \\in U, \\quad \\Delta f(x) =\n\\sum_{i=1}^n \\frac{\\partial^2 f}{\\partial x_i^2}(x)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-6\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-3-2-6\">Une fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> à valeurs réelles sur un ouvert <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> de ℝⁿ est dite harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> si<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in U, \\quad\n\\Delta f(x) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>L’ensemble des fonctions harmoniques sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> est noté <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></li>\n</ul></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.1</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques : quelques propriétés</span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-2-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> un ouvert non vide de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^n\\text{.}\\)</span> On note <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2(U,\n\\mathbb{R})\\)</span> l’espace vectoriel des fonctions de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> dans <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-3\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">1</span><span class=\"title\">La première question.</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-3-2-1\">Montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\)</span> est un sous-espace vectoriel de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2(U,\n\\mathbb{R})\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-3-3\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-3-3-1\">On a <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U) \\subset \\mathcal{C}^2(U, \\mathbb{R})\\text{.}\\)</span> La fonction nulle est harmonique. Soient <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f, g \\in \\mathcal{H}(U)\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\n\\in\n\\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f + \\lambda g\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2(U,\n\\mathbb{R})\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\forall i \\in [\\![1, n]\\!]\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial^2}{\\partial\nx_i^2}\\)</span> est linéaire, donc <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta(f + \\lambda g) = \\Delta(f) + \\lambda\n\\Delta(g) = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f + \\lambda g \\in \\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\)</span> est un sous-espace vectoriel de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2(U,\n\\mathbb{R})\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-4\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">2</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-4-1-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f \\in \\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Montrer que si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^\\infty\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{,}\\)</span> alors toute dérivée partielle à tout ordre de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> appartient à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">On suppose que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f \\in \\mathcal{C}^2(U, \\mathbb{R}) \\cap \\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(p \\in \\mathbb{N}\\text{,}\\)</span> posons <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial^p f}{\\partial x_{i_1}\n\\partial x_{i_2} \\dots \\partial x_{i_p}} = g\\text{.}\\)</span> Par application du théorème de Schwartz et de la linéarité de l’opérateur dérivée partielle, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta(g)\n= \\sum_{j=1}^n \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial x_j^2} =\n\\frac{\\partial^p}{\\partial\nx_{i_1} \\partial x_{i_2} \\dots \\partial x_{i_p}} \\left( \\sum_{j=1}^n\n\\frac{\\partial^2 f}{\\partial x_j^2} \\right) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Donc <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g \\in\n\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-5\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">3</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-5-1-1\">On suppose dans cette question que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> est connexe par arcs. Déterminer l’ensemble des fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\)</span> telles que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f^2\\)</span> appartienne aussi à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-5-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-5-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f \\in \\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{,}\\)</span> alors pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(i \\in [\\![1, n]\\!]\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial\nf^2}{\\partial x_i} = 2f \\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial x_i}\\text{.}\\)</span> En dérivant une seconde fois, on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial^2 f^2}{\\partial x_i^2} = 2\n\\left(\n\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial x_i} \\right)^2 + 2f \\frac{\\partial^2\nf}{\\partial\nx_i^2}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Donc :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta(f^2) = 0 \\iff \\sum_{i=1}^n \\left(\n\\frac{\\partial\nf}{\\partial x_i} \\right)^2 + f \\Delta(f) = 0 \\iff \\sum_{i=1}^n \\left(\n\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial x_i} \\right)^2 = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f \\in\n\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\forall i \\in [\\![1, n]\\!]\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\frac{\\partial\nf}{\\partial x_i} = 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^1\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{,}\\)</span> elle est différentiable sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{,}\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> étant connexe par arcs, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est constante sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-6\"><h5 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">4</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h5>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-6-1-1\">Donner une fonction non constante appartenant à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(U)\\text{.}\\)</span> Le produit de deux fonctions harmoniques est-il une fonction harmonique ?</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-6-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-4-6-2-1\">L’application <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f : x = (x_1, \\dots, x_n) \\mapsto x_1\\)</span> est harmonique, mais <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f^2\\)</span> ne l’est pas. Ainsi, le produit de deux fonctions harmoniques sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> n’est pas nécessairement harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Exemples de fonctions harmoniques</span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2.1</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques à variables séparables</span>\n</h5>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-2-1\">On cherche dans cette question à déterminer les fonctions harmoniques non nulles sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^2\\)</span> à variables séparables, c’est-à-dire les fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> s’écrivant sous la forme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f(x, y) = u(x)v(y)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">5</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-3-1-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Montrer qu’il existe une constante <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\)</span> réelle telle que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> soient solutions respectives des équations</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz'' + \\lambda z =\n0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz'' - \\lambda z = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\((x, y) \\in \\mathbb{R}^2\\text{,}\\)</span> on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta(f)(x, y) = u''(x)v(y)\n+ u(x)v''(y)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est harmonique, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta(f)(x, y) = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> donc :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nu''(x)v(y) + u(x)v''(y) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En divisant par <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u(x)v(y)\\)</span> (qui est non nul car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> n’est pas identiquement nulle), on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{u''(x)}{u(x)}\n+ \\frac{v''(y)}{v(y)} = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Cela implique qu’il existe une constante <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\)</span> telle que :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{u''(x)}{u(x)} = -\\lambda \\quad \\text{et}\n\\quad \\frac{v''(y)}{v(y)} = \\lambda\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> sont solutions des équations différentielles données.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">6</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-1-1\">Donner en fonction du signe de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\)</span> la forme des fonctions harmoniques à variables séparables.</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Selon le signe de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\text{,}\\)</span> les solutions sont :</div>\n<ul class=\"disc\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2\">\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-1\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-1\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de la forme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f(x, y) =\n(a x + b)(c y + d)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-2\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-2\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda \\gt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de la forme :<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x,\ny) = \\left( a \\cos(\\sqrt{\\lambda} x) + b \\sin(\\sqrt{\\lambda} x) \\right)\n\\left( c e^{\\sqrt{\\lambda} y} + d e^{-\\sqrt{\\lambda} y} \\right)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></li>\n<li id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-3\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"p-derived-centrale2018mp2-3-5-2-4-2-1-2-3\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda \\lt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de la forme :<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x,\ny) = \\left( a e^{\\sqrt{-\\lambda} x} + b e^{-\\sqrt{-\\lambda} x} \\right)\n\\left( c \\cos(\\sqrt{-\\lambda} y) + d \\sin(\\sqrt{-\\lambda} y)\n\\right)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a, b, c, d\\)</span> sont des constantes arbitraires.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2.2</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques radiales</span>\n</h5>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-2\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> une fonction réelle de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^2\n\\setminus \\{(0, 0)\\}\\text{.}\\)</span> On pose, pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\((r, \\theta) \\in \\mathbb{R}^+\n\\times \\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\ng(r, \\theta) = f(r \\cos(\\theta), r \\sin(\\theta))\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">7</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-3-1-1\">Justifier que <span \\mathbb{R}\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-3-2-1\">L’application <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h : (r, \\theta) \\mapsto (r \\cos(\\theta), r\n\\sin(\\theta))\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^+\n\\times \\mathbb{R}\\)</span> car ses composantes le sont. Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g = f \\circ\nh\\text{,}\\)</span> et que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> est également de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-4\"><h6 id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-4-1-1\">Pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\((r, \\theta) \\in \\mathbb{R}^+ \\times \\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span> exprimer <span class=\"process-math\">\\((x,\ny) = (r \\cos(\\theta), r \\sin(\\theta))\\text{.}\\)</span> Alors :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial\ng}{\\partial id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-6\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">10</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div \\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2 \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial\nr^2}(r, \\theta) + \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-6-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">En utilisant les expressions des dérivées partielles de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> obtenues précédemment, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2 \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial\nr^2}(r, \\theta) +\n\\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial \\theta^2}(r, \\theta) + r \\frac{\\partial\ng}{\\partial r}(r, \\theta) = r^2 \\Delta(f)(x, y)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\((x, y) = (r\n\\cos(\\theta), r \\sin(\\theta))\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est harmonique si et seulement si :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2 \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial r^2}(r, \\theta) +\n\\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial \\theta^2}(r, \\theta) + r \\frac{\\partial\ng}{\\partial r}(r, \\theta) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-7\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">11</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-7-1-1\">Déterminer les fonctions harmoniques radiales de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^2\n\\setminus\n\\{(0, 0)\\}\\text{,}\\)</span> c’est-à-dire les fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> appartenant à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(\\mathbb{R}^2\n\\setminus \\{(0, 0)\\})\\)</span> telles que <span class=\"process-math\">\\((r, \\theta) \\mapsto f(r\n\\cos(\\theta), r \\sin(\\theta))\\)</span> soit indépendante de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\theta\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-7-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-7-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est radiale, alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g(r, \\theta) = g(r)\\)</span> est indépendante de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\theta\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, l’équation harmonique se réduit à :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2 g''(r)\n+ r g'(r) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En posant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h(r) = g'(r)\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2\nh'(r)\n+ r h(r) = 0 \\quad \\Rightarrow \\quad h'(r) + \\frac{1}{r} h(r) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Cette équation différentielle a pour solution :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nh(r) = \\frac{C}{r}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C\\)</span> est une constante. En intégrant, on trouve :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\ng(r) = C\n\\ln(r) + D\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D\\)</span> est une autre constante. Ainsi, les fonctions harmoniques radiales sont de la forme :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x, y) = C\n\\ln(\\sqrt{x^2 + y^2}) + D\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D\\)</span> sont des constantes.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-8\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">12</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-8-1-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soient <span class=\"process-math\">\\(a, b, r_1\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(r_2\\)</span> quatre réels tels que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0 \\lt r_1\n\\lt\nr_2\\text{.}\\)</span> Déterminer une fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^2\n\\setminus \\{(0, 0)\\}\\)</span> telle que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\begin{cases}\n\\Delta f = 0 \\\\\nf(x, y) = a \\quad \\text{si } \\|(x, y)\\| = r_1 \\\\\nf(x, y) = b \\quad \\text{si } \\|(x, y)\\| = r_2\n\\end{cases}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-8-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-3-8-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">D’après la question précédente, les fonctions harmoniques radiales sont de la forme :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x, y) = C \\ln(\\sqrt{x^2 + y^2}) + D\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D\\)</span> sont des constantes. En utilisant les conditions aux limites, on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\begin{cases}\nC \\ln(r_1) + D = a \\\\\nC \\ln(r_2) + D = b\n\\end{cases}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En résolvant ce système, on trouve :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nC =\n\\frac{a -\nb}{\\ln(r_1) - \\ln(r_2)} \\quad \\text{et} \\quad D = a - C \\ln(r_1)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi, la fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est donnée par :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x, y) = \\frac{a -\nb}{\\ln(r_1) -\n\\ln(r_2)} \\ln(\\sqrt{x^2 + y^2}) + a - \\frac{a - b}{\\ln(r_1) -\n\\ln(r_2)}\n\\ln(r_1)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2.3</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques à variables polaires séparables</span>\n</h5>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-2\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Dans cette sous-partie, on considère deux fonctions de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> , <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u : \\mathbb{R}^+ \\rightarrow \\mathbb{R}\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v : \\mathbb{R}\n\\rightarrow \\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span> et on pose</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(r \\cos(\\theta), r \\sin(\\theta))\n=\nu(r) v(\\theta)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">13</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-3-1-1\">Montrer que, si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> n’est pas identiquement nulle, alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> est <span class=\"process-math\">\\(2\\pi\\)</span>-périodique.</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\((r, \\theta) \\in \\mathbb{R}^+ \\times \\mathbb{R}\\text{,}\\)</span> on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(r\n\\cos(\\theta + 2\\pi), r \\sin(\\theta + 2\\pi)) = f(r \\cos(\\theta), r\n\\sin(\\theta))\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Donc <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u(r) v(\\theta + 2\\pi) = u(r) v(\\theta)\\text{.}\\)</span> Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> n’est pas identiquement nulle, il existe <span class=\"process-math\">\\((r_0, \\theta_0)\\)</span> tel que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u(r_0) v(\\theta_0) \\neq 0\\text{,}\\)</span> ce qui implique que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v(\\theta\n+\n2\\pi) = v(\\theta)\\)</span> pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\theta\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> est <span class=\"process-math\">\\(2\\pi\\)</span> -périodique.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-4\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">14</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-4-1-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Montrer que, si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est harmonique et non identiquement nulle sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^2\n\\setminus \\{(0, 0)\\}\\text{,}\\)</span> alors il existe un réel <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\)</span> tel que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> soit solution de l’équation différentielle</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2 z''(r) + r\nz'(r) -\n\\lambda z(r) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> soit solution de l’équation différentielle</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz''(\\theta)\n+ \\lambda z(\\theta) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-4-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Par la question précédente, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2 u''(r) v(\\theta) + u(r)\nv''(\\theta) + r u'(r) v(\\theta) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En divisant par <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u(r)\nv(\\theta)\\)</span> (qui est non nul car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> n’est pas identiquement nulle), on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2\n\\frac{u''(r)}{u(r)} + r \\frac{u'(r)}{u(r)} +\n\\frac{v''(\\theta)}{v(\\theta)} =\n0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Cela implique qu’il existe une constante <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\\)</span> telle que :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr^2\n\\frac{u''(r)}{u(r)} + r \\frac{u'(r)}{u(r)} = \\lambda \\quad \\text{et}\n\\quad\n\\frac{v''(\\theta)}{v(\\theta)} = -\\lambda\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> sont solutions des équations différentielles données.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2.4</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Cas où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(2\\pi\\)</span>-périodiques de l’équation <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z''(\\theta)\n+ \\lambda z(\\theta) = 0\\)</span> lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda = 0\\)</span> ?</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-2-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-2-2-1\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> l’équation devient <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z''(\\theta) = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> dont les solutions sont de la forme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z(\\theta) = A \\theta + B\\text{.}\\)</span> Pour que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z\\)</span> soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(2\\pi\\)</span>-périodique, il faut que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(A = 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, les solutions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(2\\pi\\)</span>-périodiques sont les fonctions constantes <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z(\\theta)\n= B\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">16</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-3-1-1\">Résoudre l’équation différentielle <span class=\"process-math\">\\(r^2 z''(r) + r z'(r) = 0\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^+\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">L’équation différentielle <span class=\"process-math\">\\(r^2 z''(r) + r z'(r) = 0\\)</span> peut être réécrite sous la forme :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr z''(r) + z'(r) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En posant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(y(r)\n= z'(r)\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nr y'(r) + y(r) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Cette équation est une équation différentielle linéaire du premier ordre, dont la solution est :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\ny(r) = \\frac{C}{r}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C\\)</span> est une constante. En intégrant, on trouve :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz(r) = C \\ln(r) + D\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D\\)</span> est une autre constante. Ainsi, les solutions sont de la forme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z(r) = C\n\\ln(r) + D\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-4\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">17</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-4-1-1\">En déduire, dans le cas <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> les fonctions harmoniques à variables polaires séparables.</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-5-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Dans le cas <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> les fonctions harmoniques à variables polaires séparables sont de la forme :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(r \\cos(\\theta), r\n\\sin(\\theta)) = u(r) v(\\theta) = (C \\ln(r) + D) \\cdot B\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C\\)</span> , <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(B\\)</span> sont des constantes. Ainsi, les fonctions harmoniques sont de la forme :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x, y) = C \\ln(\\sqrt{x^2 + y^2}) + D\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D\\)</span> sont des constantes.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.2.5</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Cas où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda \\neq 0\\)</span></span>\n</h5>\n<article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-2\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">18</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-2-1-1\">Donner une condition nécessaire et suffisante pour que l’équation <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z''(\\theta)\n+ \\lambda z(\\theta) = 0\\)</span> admette des solutions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(2\\pi\\)</span>-périodiques non nulles. Donner ces solutions.</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-2-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-2-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Pour que l’équation <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z''(\\theta) + \\lambda z(\\theta) = 0\\)</span> admette des solutions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(2\\pi\\)</span>-périodiques non nulles, il faut que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\n\\gt 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Dans ce cas, les solutions sont de la forme :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz(\\theta) =\nA \\cos(\\sqrt{\\lambda} \\theta) + B \\sin(\\sqrt{\\lambda} \\theta)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(A\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(B\\)</span> sont des constantes.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">19</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-3-1-1\">Résoudre l’équation différentielle <span class=\"process-math\">\\(r^2 z''(r) + r z'(r) - \\lambda\nz(r) = 0\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^+\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">L’équation différentielle <span class=\"process-math\">\\(r^2 z''(r) + r z'(r) - \\lambda z(r) = 0\\)</span> est une équation d’Euler. En posant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z(r) = r^k\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient l’équation caractéristique :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nk(k - 1) + k - \\lambda = 0 \\quad\n\\Rightarrow \\quad k^2 - \\lambda = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Les solutions sont donc :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz(r)\n= C r^{\\sqrt{\\lambda}} + D r^{-\\sqrt{\\lambda}} \\quad \\text{si} \\quad\n\\lambda \\gt 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz(r) = C \\cos(\\sqrt{-\\lambda} \\ln(r)) + D\n\\sin(\\sqrt{-\\lambda} \\ln(r)) \\quad \\text{si} \\quad \\lambda \\lt 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(C\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D\\)</span> sont des constantes.</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-4\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">20</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-4-1-1\">Quelles sont les solutions se prolongeant par continuité en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0\\)</span> ?</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-5-6-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Pour que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z(r)\\)</span> se prolonge par continuité en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0\\text{,}\\)</span> il faut que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z(r)\\)</span> reste bornée lorsque <span class=\"process-math\">\\(r \\to 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Dans le cas <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda \\gt\n0\\text{,}\\)</span> la solution <span class=\"process-math\">\\(z(r) = C r^{\\sqrt{\\lambda}} + D\nr^{-\\sqrt{\\lambda}}\\)</span> ne reste bornée que si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D = 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, les solutions prolongeables en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0\\)</span> sont de la forme :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nz(r) = C\nr^{\\sqrt{\\lambda}} \\quad \\text{si} \\quad \\lambda \\gt 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Dans le cas <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\lambda\n\\lt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> aucune solution ne reste bornée en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.3</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Principe du maximum faible</span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-2\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> un ouvert borné non vide de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^n\\)</span> (<span class=\"process-math\">\\(n \\geq 2\\)</span>) et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\n: U \\rightarrow \\mathbb{R}\\)</span> de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\text{.}\\)</span> Le but de cette partie est de montrer le théorème suivant, connu sous le nom de principe du maximum faible :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\text{Si } f \\text{ est une fonction continue sur } \\overline{U},\n\\text{ de classe } \\mathcal{C}^2 \\text{ et harmonique sur } U, \\text{ alors }\n\\forall x \\in U, \\quad f(x) \\leq \\sup_{y \\in \\partial U} f(y)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\partial U\\)</span> désigne la frontière de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.3.1</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Cas où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta f \\gt 0\\)</span></span>\n</h5>\n<article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-2\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">21</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-2-1-1\">Montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> admet un maximum en un point <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x_0 \\in \\overline{U}\\)</span> .</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-2-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-2-2-1\">Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> est un ouvert borné, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\)</span> est un fermé borné de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}^n\\text{,}\\)</span> donc un compact. La fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est continue sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\text{,}\\)</span> donc elle est bornée et atteint ses bornes. Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> admet un maximum en un point <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x_0 \\in \\overline{U}\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">22</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-3-1-1\">On suppose de plus que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> et que, pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\in U\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta f(x) \\gt 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x_0\n\\in \\partial U\\)</span> et en déduire que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\forall x \\in U\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f(x) \\lt\n\\sup_{y \\in \\partial U} f(y)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-3-2-1\">Supposons par l’absurde que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x_0 \\in U\\text{.}\\)</span> Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> admet un maximum en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x_0\\text{,}\\)</span> toutes les dérivées partielles secondes de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x_0\\)</span> doivent être négatives ou nulles. En particulier, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta\nf(x_0) \\leq 0\\text{,}\\)</span> ce qui contredit l’hypothèse <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta f(x_0) \\gt 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x_0 \\in \\partial U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-3-3-2-2\">Par conséquent, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\forall x \\in U\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f(x) \\lt f(x_0) = \\sup_{y \\in\n\\partial U} f(y)\\text{,}\\)</span> car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> atteint son maximum sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\)</span> uniquement en des points de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\partial U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.3.2</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Cas général</span>\n</h5>\n<article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-2\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">23</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-2-1-1\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> une fonction continue sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\text{,}\\)</span> de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> et harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span> Pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\varepsilon \\gt 0\\text{,}\\)</span> on pose <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g_\\varepsilon(x) = f(x) + \\varepsilon \\|x\\|^2\\text{.}\\)</span> Montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g_\\varepsilon\\)</span> est une fonction continue sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\text{,}\\)</span> de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-2-2-1\">La fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g_\\varepsilon(x) = f(x) + \\varepsilon \\|x\\|^2\\)</span> est continue sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\)</span> car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est continue et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\|x\\|^2\\)</span> est continue. Elle est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\)</span> car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\|x\\|^2\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^\\infty\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-2-2-2\">\n<div class=\"para\">De plus, pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\in U\\text{,}\\)</span> on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta g_\\varepsilon(x) =\n\\Delta f(x) + \\varepsilon \\Delta (\\|x\\|^2) = 0 + \\varepsilon \\cdot 2n = 2n\n\\varepsilon \\gt 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">car <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est harmonique et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta (\\|x\\|^2) =\n2n\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">24</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-3-1-1\">En déduire que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\forall x \\in U\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f(x) \\leq \\sup_{y \\in \\partial U}\nf(y)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Par la question précédente, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g_\\varepsilon\\)</span> vérifie <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta\ng_\\varepsilon(x) \\gt 0\\)</span> pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\in U\\text{.}\\)</span> D’après la partie III.A, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in U, \\quad g_\\varepsilon(x) \\lt \\sup_{y \\in\n\\partial U} g_\\varepsilon(y)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En faisant tendre <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\varepsilon \\to 0\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in U, \\quad f(x) \\leq \\sup_{y \\in \\partial U}\nf(y)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-4\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">25</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-4-1-1\">Soient <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f_1\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f_2\\)</span> deux fonctions continues sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\text{,}\\)</span> de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> et harmoniques sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span> Montrer que si les fonctions <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f_1\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f_2\\)</span> sont égales sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\partial U\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f_1\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f_2\\)</span> sont égales sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-4-2-1\">Considérons la fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h = f_1 - f_2\\text{.}\\)</span> Alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h\\)</span> est continue sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\overline{U}\\text{,}\\)</span> de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{,}\\)</span> et harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span> De plus, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h = 0\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\partial U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-6-4-4-2-2\">\n<div class=\"para\">Par le principe du maximum faible, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in U, \\quad h(x)\n\\leq \\sup_{y \\in \\partial U} h(y) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall x \\in U, \\quad\n-h(x) \\leq \\sup_{y \\in \\partial U} (-h(y)) = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h(x) = 0\\)</span> pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(x \\in U\\text{,}\\)</span> c’est-à-dire <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f_1 = f_2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(U\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article></section></section><section class=\"subsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7\"><h4 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.4</span><span class=\"type\">Sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques et fonctions développables en série entière</span>\n</h4>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-2\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">On dit qu’une fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\text{,}\\)</span> définie sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R) \\subset \\mathbb{R}^2\\)</span> et à valeurs complexes, se développe en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\)</span> s’il existe une suite complexe <span class=\"process-math\">\\((a_n)\\)</span> telle que</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\forall (x, y) \\in D(0, R), \\quad f(x,\ny) = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} a_n (x + i y)^n\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Dans toute cette partie, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> désigne une fonction se développant en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.4.1</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions développables en série entière</span>\n</h5>\n<article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-2\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">26</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-2-1-1\">Montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^1\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\)</span> et que ses dérivées partielles se développent en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0,\nR)\\text{.}\\)</span> Que peut-on en déduire pour la fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> ?</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-2-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\">\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-2-2-1\">La fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> se développe en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{,}\\)</span> donc elle est analytique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span> Par conséquent, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^\\infty\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{,}\\)</span> et en particulier de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^1\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-2-2-2\">\n<div class=\"para\">Les dérivées partielles de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> sont données par :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial\nf}{\\partial x}(x, y) = \\sum_{n=1}^{+\\infty} n a_n (x + i y)^{n-1}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial\nf}{\\partial y}(x, y) = \\sum_{n=1}^{+\\infty} i n a_n (x + i y)^{n-1}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ces séries convergent également sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{,}\\)</span> donc les dérivées partielles se développent en série entière.</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-2-2-3\">On en déduit que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^\\infty\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0,\nR)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">27</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-3-1-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">On note <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> les parties réelle et imaginaire de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\text{,}\\)</span> de sorte que, quel que soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\((x, y) \\in D(0, R)\\text{,}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nu(x, y) \\in\n\\mathbb{R}, \\quad v(x, y) \\in \\mathbb{R}, \\quad f(x, y) = u(x, y) + i v(x,\ny)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Montrer que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> sont des fonctions harmoniques sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0,\nR)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-3-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">La fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est analytique, donc elle vérifie les équations de Cauchy-Riemann :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial u}{\\partial x} = \\frac{\\partial\nv}{\\partial y} \\quad \\text{et} \\quad \\frac{\\partial u}{\\partial y} =\n-\\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial x}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En dérivant ces équations, on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial^2\nu}{\\partial x^2} = \\frac{\\partial^2 v}{\\partial x \\partial y} \\quad\n\\text{et} \\quad \\frac{\\partial^2 u}{\\partial y^2} = -\\frac{\\partial^2\nv}{\\partial y \\partial x}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En additionnant ces deux équations, on trouve :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta u = \\frac{\\partial^2 u}{\\partial x^2} + \\frac{\\partial^2\nu}{\\partial y^2} = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">De même, on montre que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta v = 0\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> sont harmoniques sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.4.2</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Propriétés des fonctions développables en série entière</span>\n</h5>\n<section class=\"introduction\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-2\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">On admet le résultat suivant : une fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h\\)</span> de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\)</span> dans <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{C}\\)</span> se développe en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\)</span> si et seulement si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^1\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\)</span> et pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\((x,\ny) \\in D(0, R)\\text{,}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial h}{\\partial y}(x, y) = i \\frac{\\partial\nh}{\\partial x}(x, y)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></section><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">28</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-3-1-1\">Montrer que si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> ne s’annule pas sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(1/f\\)</span> se développe en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> ne s’annule pas sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(1/f\\)</span> est bien définie et de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^1\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span> De plus, comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> est analytique, elle vérifie les équations de Cauchy-Riemann :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial\nf}{\\partial y} = i \\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial x}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En dérivant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(1/f\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial (1/f)}{\\partial y} = -\\frac{1}{f^2}\n\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial y} = -\\frac{1}{f^2} \\left( i \\frac{\\partial\nf}{\\partial x} \\right) = i \\left( -\\frac{1}{f^2} \\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial\nx} \\right) = i \\frac{\\partial (1/f)}{\\partial x}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(1/f\\)</span> vérifie également les équations de Cauchy-Riemann et est donc développable en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-4\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">29</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-4-1-1\">Montrer que la fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u v\\)</span> est harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-4-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-4-4-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">On a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\Delta(u v) = \\Delta(u) v + u \\Delta(v) + 2 \\left( \\frac{\\partial\nu}{\\partial x} \\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial x} + \\frac{\\partial u}{\\partial y}\n\\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial y} \\right)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(v\\)</span> sont harmoniques, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta(u) = \\Delta(v) = 0\\text{.}\\)</span> De plus, les équations de Cauchy-Riemann donnent :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial u}{\\partial x} = \\frac{\\partial\nv}{\\partial y} \\quad \\text{et} \\quad \\frac{\\partial u}{\\partial y} =\n-\\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial x}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial u}{\\partial\nx} \\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial x} + \\frac{\\partial u}{\\partial y}\n\\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial y} = \\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial y} \\frac{\\partial\nv}{\\partial x} - \\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial x} \\frac{\\partial v}{\\partial y}\n= 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Donc <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\Delta(u v) = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> et <span class=\"process-math\">\\(u v\\)</span> est harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0,\nR)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.4.3</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Fonctions harmoniques et leurs primitives</span>\n</h5>\n<article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5-2\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">30</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5-2-1-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> une fonction de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R) \\subset \\mathbb{R}^2\\)</span> dans <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathbb{R}\\text{.}\\)</span> On suppose que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> est harmonique. Montrer que la fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h\\)</span> définie sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\)</span> par</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nh : (x, y) \\mapsto\n\\frac{\\partial g}{\\partial x}(x, y) - i \\frac{\\partial g}{\\partial y}(x, y)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">se développe en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5-2-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5-2-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">La fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> est harmonique, donc elle est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^2\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h\\)</span> est de classe <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{C}^1\\)</span> sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span> De plus, comme <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> est harmonique, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial x^2} + \\frac{\\partial^2\ng}{\\partial y^2} = 0\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En dérivant <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial\nh}{\\partial y} = \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial y \\partial x} - i\n\\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial y^2}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial h}{\\partial x}\n= \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial x^2} - i \\frac{\\partial^2 g}{\\partial x\n\\partial y}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En utilisant le théorème de Schwarz et l’équation harmonique, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial h}{\\partial y} = i \\frac{\\partial\nh}{\\partial x}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h\\)</span> vérifie les équations de Cauchy-Riemann et est donc développable en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">31</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5-3-1-1\">Montrer que si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> appartient à <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\mathcal{H}(D(0, R))\\text{,}\\)</span> alors il existe une fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(H\\)</span> se développant en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\)</span> telle que <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> est la partie réelle de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(H\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-5-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">D’après la question précédente, la fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(h\\)</span> définie par</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nh(x, y)\n= \\frac{\\partial g}{\\partial x}(x, y) - i \\frac{\\partial g}{\\partial y}(x,\ny)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">se développe en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span> On peut donc écrire :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nh(x, y) = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} a_n (x + i y)^n\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En intégrant terme à terme, on obtient une fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(H\\)</span> telle que :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nH(x,\ny) = \\alpha + \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} \\frac{a_n}{n+1} (x + i y)^{n+1}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">où <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\alpha\\)</span> est une constante. La partie réelle de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(H\\)</span> est alors <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\text{,}\\)</span> car :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial H}{\\partial x} = h(x, y) = \\frac{\\partial\ng}{\\partial x} - i \\frac{\\partial g}{\\partial y}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">et</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\frac{\\partial\nH}{\\partial y} = i h(x, y) = i \\frac{\\partial g}{\\partial x} +\n\\frac{\\partial g}{\\partial y}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Ainsi, <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> est la partie réelle de <span class=\"process-math\">\\(H\\text{.}\\)</span>\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article></section><section class=\"subsubsection\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6\"><h5 class=\"heading hide-type\">\n<span class=\"codenumber\">1.1.4.4</span><span class=\"type\">Sous sous-section</span><span class=\"title\">Propriétés intégrales des fonctions harmoniques</span>\n</h5>\n<article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-2\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">32</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-2-1-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Montrer que pour tout <span class=\"process-math\">\\(r \\in [0, R[\\text{,}\\)</span> on a</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(0) = \\frac{1}{2\\pi}\n\\int_0^{2\\pi} f(r \\cos(t), r \\sin(t)) \\, dt\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-2-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-2-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">La fonction <span class=\"process-math\">\\(f\\)</span> se développe en série entière sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{,}\\)</span> donc on peut écrire :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(x, y) = \\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} a_n (x + i y)^n\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En coordonnées polaires, on a :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(r \\cos(t), r \\sin(t)) =\n\\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} a_n r^n e^{i n t}\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">En intégrant sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\([0, 2\\pi]\\text{,}\\)</span> on obtient :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\int_0^{2\\pi} f(r \\cos(t), r \\sin(t)) \\, dt =\n\\sum_{n=0}^{+\\infty} a_n r^n \\int_0^{2\\pi} e^{i n t} \\, dt\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Or, pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n\n\\neq 0\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\int_0^{2\\pi} e^{i n t} \\, dt = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> et pour <span class=\"process-math\">\\(n = 0\\text{,}\\)</span> <span class=\"process-math\">\\(\\int_0^{2\\pi}\ne^{i 0 t} \\, dt = 2\\pi\\text{.}\\)</span> Ainsi :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\n\\int_0^{2\\pi} f(r \\cos(t), r\n\\sin(t)) \\, dt = 2\\pi a_0 = 2\\pi f(0)\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n<div class=\"para\">Donc :</div>\n<div class=\"displaymath process-math\">\n\\begin{equation*}\nf(0) =\n\\frac{1}{2\\pi}\n\\int_0^{2\\pi} f(r \\cos(t), r \\sin(t)) \\, dt\n\\end{equation*}\n</div>\n</div></div></details></div></article><article class=\"exercise exercise-like\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-3\"><h6 class=\"heading\">\n<span class=\"before\"> </span><span class=\"codenumber\">33</span><span class=\"after\"> </span><span class=\"space\"> </span>\n</h6>\n<div class=\"para\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-3-1-1\">Montrer un résultat analogue pour les fonctions harmoniques.</div>\n<div class=\"solutions\"><details id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-3-2\" class=\"solution solution-like born-hidden-knowl\"><summary class=\"knowl__link\"><span class=\"type\">Solution</span></summary><div class=\"solution solution-like knowl__content\"><div class=\"para logical\" id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-3-2-1\">\n<div class=\"para\">Soit <span class=\"process-math\">\\(g\\)</span> une fonction harmonique sur <span class=\"process-math\">\\(D(0, R)\\text{.}\\)</span> D’après la question précédente, il existe une fonction <span id=\"centrale2018mp2-3-7-6-6-1-1\">Montrer que si <span class=\"process-math\">\\(|f|\\)</span> admet un maximum en <span class=\"process-math\">\\(0\\text{,}\\)</span> alors <span
